I know that may not totally answer what you’re asking, but that’s what it keeps coming back to.\n\nDoctor: Was the event something that involved you directly experiencing or witnessing something very serious, like actual or threatened death, injury, or something similar?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure how to answer that the right way. It started after my husband fell while we were repairing the porch.\n\nI’m sorry, that’s probably what I should have said sooner. It’s been about four months now since that happened, and I’ve just… I haven’t really felt back to normal since.\n\nDoctor: Since your husband’s fall, have you noticed that these feelings of fear and guilt have been constant, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: It’s… more constant than I’d like to admit. I mean, I have moments where I can function and seem okay, so I don’t want to make it sound like I’m falling apart every second.\n\nBut what brings me in is that I can’t really shake this heavy mix of fear and guilt. I feel tense a lot of the day, like I’m waiting for something else to go wrong, and I keep blaming myself for not stopping what happened. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Since your husband’s fall, have you felt a persistent sense of fear or guilt that hasn’t gone away, even when nothing else seemed to be happening?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s a fair way to put it.\n\nI don’t want to overstate it, because I am still getting through the day, but it hasn’t really gone away. There’s this fear and guilt that just sits there, even when nothing is actually happening. I feel tense, like I’m waiting for something else to go wrong, and then I keep going back to blaming myself for not stopping it.\n\nI’m sorry if I’m repeating myself. Yes… I mean, not constantly, but it does happen.

I don’t want to sound dramatic.\n\nWhat brings me in is mostly that since then I just can’t seem to get rid of this heavy mix of fear and guilt. I’m tense almost all day, like I’m waiting for something else to go wrong, and I keep blaming myself for not stopping it somehow. I know that may not totally answer what you’re asking, but that’s what it keeps coming back to.\n\nDoctor: Was the event something that involved you directly experiencing or witnessing something very serious, like actual or threatened death, injury, or something similar?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure how to answer that the right way. Was it something that happened to you directly, or something you saw or heard about that really affected you?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry… I know you’re trying to understand, and I’m probably not being very helpful.\n\nI don’t know that I can give a clear answer yet. I don’t think so… not really. I mean, I still feel love for my family, and there are moments where I feel happy, or at least comforted.

Maybe I’m a little more tired or distracted sometimes, so it doesn’t always hit the same way, but I wouldn’t say I can’t feel those things. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m shut off emotionally, because I don’t think that’s true. Does that answer what you’re asking?

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF43.22, F32.1, F32.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, thank you for coming in. Can you tell me what’s been troubling you lately that brought you here today?\n\nPatient: I guess… lately I just haven’t been able to shake this really heavy feeling. It’s like fear and guilt kind of sitting on me all day. I’m probably not explaining it very well, but I feel tense a lot, like I’m waiting for something else to go wrong, and I keep blaming myself for not stopping what happened. I tried to just push through it, but it’s been hard.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about a lot of different things, even when there isn’t a clear reason to be concerned? Like everyday responsibilities, work, health, or other things?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s a lot of different things exactly… I mean, I do worry, but it’s more like this constant heavy feeling, if that makes sense. Like fear and guilt sitting there most of the day. I’m tense a lot, kind of waiting for something to go wrong, and I keep thinking I should’ve stopped what happened somehow. I know that may not be the kind of worry you mean.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about the guilt you mentioned earlier — is it related to a specific event or situation that’s been on your mind?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it is tied to what happened. I don’t really like saying it too plainly, because I know blaming myself probably isn’t… helpful, but it’s like I keep going back to this feeling that I should’ve somehow stopped it.\n\nIt’s not that I’m sitting there making a whole argument in my head all day, exactly. It’s more this heavy mix of fear and guilt that just stays with me. I feel tense, like I’m waiting for something else to go wrong, and then I keep thinking maybe I missed something or didn’t do enough. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what happened — was there a specific event or situation that this fear and guilt are centered around?\n\nPatient: It was… yeah, it was a specific thing, but I’m sorry, I’m still kind of having a hard time saying it straight out. I don’t want to sound dramatic.\n\nWhat brings me in is mostly that since then I just can’t seem to get rid of this heavy mix of fear and guilt. I’m tense almost all day, like I’m waiting for something else to go wrong, and I keep blaming myself for not stopping it somehow. I know that may not totally answer what you’re asking, but that’s what it keeps coming back to.\n\nDoctor: Was the event something that involved you directly experiencing or witnessing something very serious, like actual or threatened death, injury, or something similar?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure how to answer that the right way. It felt really serious to me, but I don’t want to overstate it or say it fits something if it doesn’t.\n\nI guess I’m still trying to sort out what counts, if that makes sense. I can say it shook me up, but I’m having a hard time being more specific than that right now.\n\nDoctor: Can you try to share a bit more about the event that has been causing you to feel this way? Was it something that happened to you directly, or something you saw or heard about that really affected you?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry… I know you’re trying to understand, and I’m probably not being very helpful.\n\nI don’t know that I can give a clear answer yet. It wasn’t like I came here with the details all sorted out to say neatly. I can say it affected me, and I keep circling back to it, but when I try to say whether it was directly me or something I saw or heard about, I kind of freeze up.\n\nIs it okay if I just say that much for now?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if this feeling started after something happened that was really upsetting or traumatic for you?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. It started after my husband fell while we were repairing the porch.\n\nI’m sorry, that’s probably what I should have said sooner. It’s been about four months now since that happened, and I’ve just… I haven’t really felt back to normal since.\n\nDoctor: Since your husband’s fall, have you noticed that these feelings of fear and guilt have been constant, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: It’s… more constant than I’d like to admit. I mean, I have moments where I can function and seem okay, so I don’t want to make it sound like I’m falling apart every second.\n\nBut what brings me in is that I can’t really shake this heavy mix of fear and guilt. I feel tense a lot of the day, like I’m waiting for something else to go wrong, and I keep blaming myself for not stopping what happened. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Since your husband’s fall, have you felt a persistent sense of fear or guilt that hasn’t gone away, even when nothing else seemed to be happening?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s a fair way to put it.\n\nI don’t want to overstate it, because I am still getting through the day, but it hasn’t really gone away. There’s this fear and guilt that just sits there, even when nothing is actually happening. I feel tense, like I’m waiting for something else to go wrong, and then I keep going back to blaming myself for not stopping it.\n\nI’m sorry if I’m repeating myself. That’s just… the part I can’t seem to shake.\n\nDoctor: Since your husband’s fall, have you found yourself avoiding certain places, people, or activities that remind you of what happened?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. I mean, I try not to make a big deal of it, but I’ve noticed I’ll turn down certain jobs now if they involve raised porches or second-story decks. I tell myself it’s just scheduling or that someone else is better suited for it, but… I think I know why I’m doing it.\n\nAnd at home, I kind of avoid that area if I can. If the repair comes up, I’ll change the subject or find something else to do. I don’t know if that counts as avoiding, but it probably does, doesn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Since your husband’s fall, have you noticed that your fear and guilt are focused mostly on specific memories or reminders of the event, or do they seem to come up more generally, even without thinking about the fall?\n\nPatient: I think it’s both, but… more general than I expected, if that makes sense.\n\nCertain reminders definitely set it off, but what bothers me is that even when I’m not actively thinking about the fall, I still feel this heavy mix of fear and guilt sitting there. Like I’m tense most of the day, waiting for something else to go wrong, and then my mind goes back to, “I should’ve stopped it somehow.”\n\nI know that may not be the clearest answer, but that’s how it feels.\n\nDoctor: Since your husband’s fall, have you noticed that you’ve lost interest in things you used to care about or enjoy, like hobbies, work, or time with friends and family?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest, exactly. I mean, I still care about work and my family, and I still try to show up for people.\n\nMaybe I’m a little less enthusiastic sometimes, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing as losing interest. I haven’t really thought of it that way. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Since your husband’s fall, have you noticed that you’re not feeling joy or pleasure in the same things that used to bring you happiness, even temporarily?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way I’ve really noticed. I mean, things may feel a little more muted sometimes, but I can still enjoy parts of my day, like being with family or getting through a job well.\n\nSo I wouldn’t want to overstate it. I’m not sure if that’s what you mean, but I don’t think I’ve stopped feeling pleasure altogether.\n\nDoctor: Since your husband’s fall, have you had upsetting thoughts or memories about it that keep coming up, even when you don’t want them to?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, not constantly, but it does happen.\n\nI’ll be doing something normal at work, like measuring a railing, or I’ll hear a ladder scrape, and then out of nowhere I can picture him falling like it’s happening right in front of me. It’s not like I’m trying to think about it. My stomach just drops and my hands get shaky.\n\nI know that probably sounds dramatic, and I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but… yes, those memories do come up when I don’t want them to.\n\nDoctor: Since your husband’s fall, have you found it hard to feel happiness or love, even during moments that used to bring you joy?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really. I mean, I still feel love for my family, and there are moments where I feel happy, or at least comforted.\n\nMaybe I’m a little more tired or distracted sometimes, so it doesn’t always hit the same way, but I wouldn’t say I can’t feel those things. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m shut off emotionally, because I don’t think that’s true. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

I can say it affected me, and I keep circling back to it, but when I try to say whether it was directly me or something I saw or heard about, I kind of freeze up.\n\nIs it okay if I just say that much for now?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if this feeling started after something happened that was really upsetting or traumatic for you?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. It started after my husband fell while we were repairing the porch.\n\nI’m sorry, that’s probably what I should have said sooner. It’s been about four months now since that happened, and I’ve just… I haven’t really felt back to normal since.\n\nDoctor: Since your husband’s fall, have you noticed that these feelings of fear and guilt have been constant, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: It’s… more constant than I’d like to admit. I mean, I have moments where I can function and seem okay, so I don’t want to make it sound like I’m falling apart every second.\n\nBut what brings me in is that I can’t really shake this heavy mix of fear and guilt. I feel tense a lot of the day, like I’m waiting for something else to go wrong, and I keep blaming myself for not stopping what happened. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Since your husband’s fall, have you felt a persistent sense of fear or guilt that hasn’t gone away, even when nothing else seemed to be happening?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s a fair way to put it.\n\nI don’t want to overstate it, because I am still getting through the day, but it hasn’t really gone away. There’s this fear and guilt that just sits there, even when nothing is actually happening. I feel tense, like I’m waiting for something else to go wrong, and then I keep going back to blaming myself for not stopping it.\n\nI’m sorry if I’m repeating myself. That’s just… the part I can’t seem to shake.\n\nDoctor: Since your husband’s fall, have you found yourself avoiding certain places, people, or activities that remind you of what happened?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. I mean, I try not to make a big deal of it, but I’ve noticed I’ll turn down certain jobs now if they involve raised porches or second-story decks. I tell myself it’s just scheduling or that someone else is better suited for it, but… I think I know why I’m doing it.\n\nAnd at home, I kind of avoid that area if I can. If the repair comes up, I’ll change the subject or find something else to do. I don’t know if that counts as avoiding, but it probably does, doesn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Since your husband’s fall, have you noticed that your fear and guilt are focused mostly on specific memories or reminders of the event, or do they seem to come up more generally, even without thinking about the fall?\n\nPatient: I think it’s both, but… more general than I expected, if that makes sense.\n\nCertain reminders definitely set it off, but what bothers me is that even when I’m not actively thinking about the fall, I still feel this heavy mix of fear and guilt sitting there.
